    [
      "Duration of Employment Contract (Article D-1)",
      "An employment contract is entered into: for an indefinite period; or for a definite period with a view to an employment contract for an indefinite period; or for a definite period."
    ],
    [
      "Employment Contract for Indefinite Period (Article D-2)",
      "The employee commences employment for an indefinite period unless Article D-3 or Article D-4 applies."
    ],
    [
      "Employment Contract for Definite Period with View to Indefinite Period (Article D-3)",
      "The employee can commence employment for a definite period with a view to an employment contract for an indefinite period. The duration of the employment contract for a definite period referred to in the first paragraph is a maximum of the duration of the employee's induction program. The employer formulates policy for the induction of newly commencing employees in consultation with the PMR. The policy contains safeguards to ensure that new employees are adequately inducted within a maximum period of three years. The employee, as referred to in paragraph 1 of this article, is appointed for an indefinite period after the expiry of the period for which the employment contract was entered into, unless the last appraisal, as referred to in Hoofdstuk N, shows that the employee's performance is insufficient or compelling business interests, either at hogeschool level or at the level of the study program, to be demonstrated by the employer, oppose the conversion. At least four months before the expiry of the temporary employment contract, the employer indicates whether the employment contract will be continued for an indefinite period. If, due to the employee's illness, the appraisal could not take place, the originally definite period of the employment contract is extended in consultation between employer and employee by a maximum of six months to give the employer the opportunity to still appraise the employee's performance."
    ],
    [
      "Employment Contract for Definite Period (Article D-4)",
      "The employee can commence employment for a definite period, with due observance of the maximum duration as referred to in Article D-5. For an employee in the function of lector/associate lector in scale 14 and higher, the maximum duration from Article D-5 can be deviated from in the first employment contract. For an employee who works as a researcher, or an employee who exclusively replaces such an employee, if there is external funding, the maximum duration of Article D-5 can be deviated from in the first employment contract. The maximum duration of the employment contract is linked to the duration of the funding. For an employee who is exclusively assigned activities in the context of specific projects designated with the consent of the PMR, if additional funds have been made available, the maximum duration of Article D-5 can be deviated from in the first employment contract. The maximum duration of the employment contract is linked to the duration of the specific project."
    ],
    [
      "Successive Employment Contracts (Article D-5)",
      "Employment contracts for a definite period at the hogeschool and/or employer in accordance with the CAO, with the exception of temporary agency or secondment contracts, can succeed each other, whereby the maximum duration of the sum of the successive employment contracts for a definite period, in deviation from Article 7:668a Burgerlijk Wetboek, is: a. four years for teaching staff with teaching duties in arts education if the employee is also a performing professional and the scope of their employment at the employer is less than or equal to an employment size of 0.4 FTE; within this period, it is possible to conclude six employment contracts; b. for the duration of a PhD research; the number of employment contracts cannot be deviated from. The maximum remains three; c. two years for cases not falling under sub a and sub b; within this period, it is possible to agree on three employment contracts. For an employment contract for a definite period with a duration longer than one year, the possibility of interim termination is included. The notice period for employer and employee is two months. If, due to successive employment contracts, a continued employment relationship for a definite period has arisen, as referred to in Article 7:668a Burgerlijk Wetboek, no notice is required for its termination. In accordance with Article 7:668a Burgerlijk Wetboek, employment relationships between the same parties that have succeeded each other with intervals of no more than six months are deemed to form a continued employment relationship."
    ],
    [
      "Expansion of Employment (Article D-6)",
      "The scope of employment of an employee with whom, in addition to their existing activities, extra activities of an incidental and/or structural nature are agreed upon, is temporarily expanded for the duration of those activities, effective from the day on which they are actually assigned these activities. They are notified of this temporary expansion in writing."
    ],
    [
      "Suspension (Article P-1)",
      "Suspension is any temporary relief from the full or partial exercise of an employee's function that is designated as such by the employer. Suspension is carried out based on a decision to that effect by the employer and can take effect immediately. During the suspension, the employee only has access to the hogeschool after obtaining permission from the employer. The employer can suspend the employee in cases where the interest of the employer requires this, for a maximum of 3 months, with the understanding that this period can be extended once by a maximum of 3 months if the reason for the suspension still persists. The employer notifies the employee immediately, but no later than within two days after the decision to suspend has been taken, in writing and with reasons."
    ],
    [
      "Procedure for Disciplinary Measures (Article P-2)",
      "If the employer imposes or considers imposing a decision to suspend, the employer notifies the employee in writing and with reasons of their decision or intention to do so, as well as the procedure to be followed. If circumstances are such that the suspension must take effect immediately, the employer acts in accordance with the provisions of the first sentence during the suspension. The employee is given the opportunity to defend themselves. This defense must take place within three weeks after the written notification as referred to in the first paragraph. If the suspension takes effect immediately, the defense takes place in a corresponding manner during the suspension. The employer can uphold a decision to suspend after the defense referred to in the second paragraph has taken place or after the employee has indicated that they waive their right to defend themselves. The decision must be taken within 14 days after the defense and must be communicated to the employee in writing and with reasons. This communication must indicate how the employee can appeal against the said decision."
    ],
    [
      "Disciplinary Measures (Article P-3)",
      "The employer can impose a disciplinary measure on an employee who fails to do or omits what a good employee should do or omit in similar circumstances and/or who is guilty of dereliction of duty. The employer can take the following disciplinary measures against the employee: a. written reprimand; b. transfer; c. suspension. If the employer imposes or considers imposing a disciplinary measure, Article P-2 applies mutatis mutandis."
    ],
    [
      "Rehabilitation (Article P-4)",
      "If it appears that the disciplinary measure was wrongly imposed, the employer will ensure appropriate rehabilitation of the employee, unless the employee indicates that they do not wish rehabilitation."

    [
      "End of Employment Contract (Article Q-1)",
      "The employment contract for an indefinite period ends with notice by one of the parties with due observance of Article Q-2. The employment contract ends without notice being required: a. by mutual consent; b. at the moment the employee reaches the AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd, unless employer and employee agree otherwise; c. by the expiry of the term or by the termination of the activities for which the agreement was entered into. Under Article 7:668 BW, there is a notice period of 1 month. If there is an extension of the agreement based on Article D-6 or D-7, this extension ends without notice by the expiry of the term or by the termination of the activities for which the extension of the agreement was entered into; d. by termination for urgent reasons, as referred to in Articles 7:678 and 7:679 of the Burgerlijk Wetboek; e. during the probation period as referred to in Article 7:652 of the Burgerlijk Wetboek immediately without stating reasons, if the employee or the employer indicates this; f. in case of the employee's death; g. the moment at which the employee, who participates in the SOP scheme under Bijlage XIII or the Work Time Reduction for Seniors scheme from Article M-2, has committed to resignation in accordance with the provisions of that chapter, for the part of their employment agreed upon in the context of that scheme."
    ],
    [
      "Notice of Termination (Article Q-2)",
      "Termination of an employment relationship as referred to in Article Q-1 paragraph 1 can only be effected by the employer due to: a. the loss of jobs as a result of the termination of the company's activities or the necessary loss of jobs, viewed over a future period of at least 26 weeks, as a result of measures taken for efficient business operations due to business economic circumstances; b. illness or defects of the employee making them unable to perform the agreed work, provided that the period referred to in Article 670, paragraphs 1 and 11 of the Burgerlijk Wetboek, has expired and it is plausible that no recovery will occur within 26 weeks and that the agreed work cannot be performed in an adapted form within that period; c. the regular inability to perform the agreed work due to illness or defects of the employee with unacceptable consequences for business operations, provided that the regular inability to perform the agreed work is not the result of insufficient care by the employer for the employee's working conditions and it is plausible that no recovery will occur within 26 weeks and that the agreed work cannot be performed in an adapted form within that period; d. the unsuitability of the employee to perform the agreed work, other than as a result of illness or defects of the employee, provided that the employer has notified the employee thereof in time and has given them sufficient opportunity to improve their performance and the unsuitability is not the result of insufficient care by the employer for the employee's training or for the employee's working conditions; e. reprehensible conduct or omission by the employee, such that the employer cannot reasonably be expected to continue the employment contract; f. the employee's refusal to perform the agreed work due to a serious conscientious objection, provided that it is plausible that the agreed work cannot be performed in an adapted form; g. a disturbed employment relationship, such that the employer cannot reasonably be expected to continue the employment contract; h. the AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd. This applies to the continuation of the employment contract in accordance with Article Q-1, paragraph 2b; i. circumstances other than those mentioned above that are such that the employer cannot reasonably be expected to continue the employment contract. The notice period for both the employer and the employee is three months and is extended for the employer by one week for each full year during which the employee has been employed by the employer after reaching the age of 45, with a maximum of 13 weeks, unless employer and employee agree otherwise in mutual consultation at the time of termination. For termination of the employment contract due to AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd, a period of one month applies. Notwithstanding paragraph 2, for termination referring to paragraph 1c, a notice period of one month applies, and for termination referring to paragraph 1b, the statutory notice periods apply, unless there is a disability or chronic illness. In that case, the notice period from paragraph 2 applies. Notice must be given in writing. The effective date of dismissal as referred to in this article is the first of the calendar month, unless otherwise agreed."

      "End of Income (Article Q-3)",
      "The employee is entitled to income, subject to unpaid special leave and with due observance of the provisions of the ZAHBO, until the day on which the employment contract ends. Any remaining vacation leave should be taken as much as possible in the period between the date of notice and the date on which the employment contract ends. If the employee is still entitled to vacation leave at the end of the employment relationship, they are entitled to a payment in money equal to the amount of income for a period equal to the remaining vacation leave."

      "Employment after Partial Disability (Article Q-4)",
      "Upon termination of an employment contract due to unsuitability based on physical or psychological causes as referred to in Article Q-2 first paragraph under b, the employee is entitled to a subsequent employment contract for an indefinite period in another function in accordance with the provisions of the ZAHBO at the hogeschool, unless the employer demonstrates that such a function is not available and will not become available within a reasonable period. The employer engages internal or external expertise specialized in the re-integration of partially disabled persons. If, after re-examination in the context of the WAO/WIA, the employee is classified in a lower disability class, they will be offered an increase in the number of hours, unless there is a compelling service interest. A compelling service interest exists in any case if that increase leads to serious financial or organizational problems."

    [
      "Notification of Reorganization (Article R-1)",
      "The employer notifies the PMR and the parties in the local CAO consultation in writing of the intended decision to reorganize. An intended decision to reorganize is understood to mean the written intention to reorganize in which the employer indicates the motives, the consequences for the employees, and the proposed measures in response thereto. With this intended decision, a plan is submitted describing the intended change in the organization and providing a global overview of: the necessity for the reorganization; the consequences for the quantitative staffing; the expected consequences for the personnel involved in the reorganization; the proposed measures in response to these consequences and the motives behind these proposed measures; the procedure to be followed for the preparation and implementation of the reorganization."
    ],
    [
      "Reorganization (Article R-2)",
      "Reorganization is understood to mean the following: the termination of the activities of the hogeschool or of a significant part thereof due to business economic or other reasons; a significant reduction, expansion or other change of the activities of the hogeschool or of a significant part thereof; a significant change in the organization of the hogeschool or of a significant part thereof; a significant change in the distribution of powers, entering into, changing or terminating lasting cooperation with another hogeschool or the introduction or change of a significant technological facility insofar as this has far-reaching consequences for a significant number of persons working in the hogeschool; the change of the place where the hogeschool carries out its activities. The local consultation between CAO parties will aim to reach agreement on how to deal with employment conditions consequences of the reorganization. These agreements will be registered by the employer as a CAO agreement with the Minister of SZW, directie Uitvoeringstaken Arbeidsvoorwaardenwetgeving (UAW). The consultation with the PMR about the intended decision is conducted on the basis of the prevailing regulations regarding the organization of co-determination and existing agreements on form and content."
    ],
    [
      "Consultation on Reorganization (Article R-3)",
      "The notification of the reorganization as described in Article R-1 forms the basis for the consultation in the local CAO consultation. This notification of the reorganization is explained by the employer. Trade unions are given the opportunity to express their views on the intention. The employer will include these views, insofar as they relate to the nature and reason for the reorganization, in the consultation to be held with the PMR. The local CAO consultation will aim to establish a social plan to guide the social consequences of the reorganization. The proposed measures included in the plan submitted with the notification to address the expected consequences for the employees concerned form the basis for this consultation. Social consequences in this context are understood to mean changes in employment conditions, nature, content and standplaats of the function, and changes in legal status."
    ],
    [
      "Social Plan (Article R-4)",
      "The content of a social plan will depend on the nature and content of the intended reorganization. If the intended reorganization is aimed at a significant reduction in employment, the measures in the social plan will primarily be aimed at preventing forced unemployment. The social plan will include a package of agreements with possible components including: re-placement; temporary function fulfillment; re-training, up-skilling and further training; outplacement and other forms of external support to increase opportunities in the labor market; measures to remove barriers to natural attrition; the dismissal order to be applied if forced dismissals are unavoidable; the period over which the measures extend. The local CAO consultation will aim to finalize the consultation on the social plan to be applied within three months after notification of the reorganization. Parties can jointly request assistance or mediation from the national CAO consultation. The agreements from the Social Plan will be registered by the employer as a CAO agreement with the Minister of SZW, directie Uitvoeringstaken Arbeidsvoorwaardenwetgeving (UAW)."
    ],
    [
      "Sectoral Dismissal Committee and Business Economic Dismissal (Article S-1)",
      "Parties to this CAO establish an independent dismissal committee, as referred to in Article 7:671a paragraph 2 BW. The dismissal committee is housed at Stichting Onderwijsgeschillen in Utrecht. The dismissal committee is authorized to grant the employer permission to terminate the employment contract on the grounds of Article 7:669 paragraph 3 under a BW (business economic dismissal) instead of the UWV. An employer who wishes to terminate the employment contract with an employee on the grounds of Article 7:669 paragraph 3 under a BW (business economic dismissal) must submit a prior request for permission to the dismissal committee. The dismissal committee includes in its assessment the dismissal criteria as established in the Social Plan applicable to the relevant dismissal, which has been registered as a CAO with the Minister of SZW, directie Uitvoeringstaken Arbeidsvoorwaardenwetgeving (UAW). If no dismissal criteria have been established, the dismissal criteria of paragraph 4 of the Ontslagregeling apply. The 'Reglement ontslagcommissie' published on www.onderwijsgeschillen.nl applies to the dismissal committee."
    ],
    [
      "Personnel Appeal Committee (Article S-2)",
      "Parties to this CAO establish an appeal committee. The appeal committee is housed at Stichting Onderwijsgeschillen in Utrecht. Every employee whose interests are directly affected can lodge an appeal with the committee against a decision taken by or on behalf of the employer that entails: a. suspension; b. a disciplinary measure, not being dismissal. The appeal must be submitted to the committee within six weeks, calculated from the day after the day on which the decision against which the appeal is lodged was sent to the employee or handed over to them in person. In an appeal pending before the committee in which the employee's interest requires an immediate provisional measure, the employee can, by means of a reasoned request, ask the chairperson of the committee for a provisional measure, pending the decision in the main case. The ruling of the appeal committee is binding for both the employee and the employer. The regulations of the hbo appeal committee published on www.onderwijsgeschillen.nl apply to the appeal committee and the procedure before the committee."
    ],
    [
      "Flexible Formation (Article V-3)",
      "The development of the qualitative and quantitative formation will be discussed annually in the local CAO consultation of CAO parties. The employer endeavors to prevent flexible employment contracts (including on-call contracts and similar), which place the employee in a disadvantageous position, as much as possible. To prevent undesirable consequences of freelance agreements, the employer can use so-called 'nul-uren' contracts. A 'nul-uren' contract is an employment contract where the employee is available on call. A characteristic is that the employer gives no guarantee that the employee will be called up. The employer accounts for the use of so-called 'min-max' contracts in the social annual report, stating the number of contracts and the bandwidth (difference between the minimum and maximum number of hours) of the contracts. A 'min-max' contract is an employment contract where the employee is available on call. A characteristic is that the employer guarantees a certain number of hours of work (minimum x and maximum y hours per month)."
    ],
    [
      "Contract Forms and Budget (Article V3-a)",
      "The employer, as part of the main lines of the budget, determines in consultation with the PMR which contract forms other than Article D-2 and Article D-3 are necessary for the proper functioning of the hogeschool. The employer, in consultation with the PMR, sets an upper limit for the percentage of work that may be performed under a contract other than those from Article D-2 and D-3. The PMR has the right of consent on the main lines of the budget."

    [
      "Opening Hours (Article G-1)",
      "The employee performs their work activities during the hours the hogeschool is open, namely: a. on Monday to Friday between 07:00 and 22:30; b. on Saturday between 07:00 and 18:00. If this necessarily follows from the nature of the work activities, the opening hours as referred to in the previous paragraph can be deviated from."
    ],
    [
      "Working Hours Scheme (Article G-2)",
      "The employer agrees on a working hours scheme with the PMR. Within this, agreements are made based on the provisions in Hoofdstuk J. The work activities are evenly distributed over the year or contract period, respectively. The employer records in writing on which days and parts of days the employee will work. These days or parts of days can be changed in consultation. The employee receives written confirmation of such a change. Agreements on task load policy and changes thereto are submitted for approval to the PMR at the level at which the policy has been established."
    ],
    [
      "Maximum Workday (Article G-3)",
      "A workday consists of a maximum of ten as much as possible consecutive hours, interrupted by sufficient rest periods. The legally prescribed rest periods, as referred to in the Arbeidstijdenwet, do not count as working time. If there are coffee or tea breaks, these count as working time. The employer determines the daily working hours after consultation with the employee, whereby as a rule no more than eight hours are worked per day."
    ],
    [
      "Rest Days (Article G-4)",
      "The employee is entitled to at least two consecutive rest days per week."
    ],
    [
      "Working Hours for Performing Artists (Article G-5)",
      "For performing artists, a working hours scheme applies that offers possibilities to practice their profession during normal working hours if necessary."
    ],
    [
      "Working Hours for Older Employees (Article G-6)",
      "An employee aged 50 or older can be assigned evening work for a maximum of two half-days per week and 40 half-days per year due to new evening work resulting from expansion or change of the employer's activities. If the employee agrees, this maximum can be deviated from. If an employee aged 50 or older is already deployed for 40 half-days or more, this cannot be expanded, unless the employee agrees."
    ],
    [
      "Daily Working Hours (Article J-3)",
      "Based on the employee's actual weekly working hours, the employer determines the daily working hours after consultation with the employee, taking into account the employee's preference and fitting the nature of the work activities. The working hours pattern must be in accordance with the provisions of the Arbeidstijdenwet. There must be an even distribution of hours over the week. The starting point here is that the work week is filled with days of 8 hours or a half-day of 4 hours. A standard workday is a workday of 8 hours."
    ],
    [
      "Overtime Compensation (Article H-7)",
      "An employee whose salary is determined according to one of the scales 1 to 10 and who performs overtime at the employer's instruction, is granted compensation, subject to the third paragraph of this article. Overtime is understood to mean work performed outside the employee's fixed daily working hours, insofar as this exceeds these working hours. For overtime performed for less than one hour immediately following the fixed daily working hours, no compensation is granted. Overtime can only be performed at the employer's instruction. The compensation for overtime, unless it concerns irregular service as referred to in Article H-8, consists of: a. leave, equal to the number of hours exceeding the employee's fixed daily working hours, and b. a monetary amount, which for each hour of that excess amounts to a percentage of the employee's applicable hourly salary, with the understanding that the monetary amount for overtime on Monday to Friday between 08:00 and 18:00 is only granted insofar as the performance of overtime exceeds the working hours for a normbetrekking. The compensation is granted as soon as possible, but generally no later than in the calendar month following that in which the excess occurred, taking into account the employee's wishes as much as possible. Article G-2 applies here. The employer can determine that, instead of granting leave as referred to in the fourth paragraph under a of this article, a monetary amount equal to the employee's applicable hourly salary is granted for each hour. The percentage referred to in the fourth paragraph under b amounts to:",
      "Table: Overtime Compensation Percentages",
      "Headers: Overtime performed, Monday to Friday, Saturday and Sunday",
      "Data: [[\"between 00:00 and 06:00\", \"50%\", \"100%\"], [\"between 06:00 and 22:00\", \"25% (50% if >2 hours overtime after first two hours)\", \"50%\"], [\"between 22:00 and 24:00\", \"50%\", \"100%\"]]",
      "Additional Information: The percentage is 100% if the overtime is performed on a non-workday, not being a Sunday, or on the following day between 00:00 and 06:00."
    ],
    [
      "Irregular Service Allowance (Article H-8)",
      "An employee not belonging to the teaching staff whose salary is determined according to one of the scales 1 to 10 and who regularly or fairly regularly performs work at the employer's instruction at times other than on Monday to Friday between 08:00 and 18:00, is granted an allowance. The allowance amounts to a percentage of the employee's applicable hourly salary per hour worked, namely: a. 20% for the hours on Monday to Friday between 06:00 and 08:00 and between 18:00 and 22:00; b. 40% for the hours on Saturday between 06:00 and 22:00; c. 40% for the hours on Monday to Saturday between 00:00 and 06:00 and between 22:00 and 24:00; d. 65% for the hours on non-workdays, with the understanding that the mentioned percentages are calculated over a maximum of the hourly salary derived from the maximum salary belonging to scale 6. For the hours mentioned in the second paragraph under a, the allowance is only granted if the employee started their work activities before 07:00 or ended them after 20:00."

      "Professionalization and Sustainable Employability (Preambule 4)",
      "Social partners consider it important that employees continue to develop their skills and competencies, given the rapidly changing needs and demands of society, hogeschool, and students. Mobility - internal and external - is important in this regard, as are adaptability and the willingness to work in changing functions and roles. The employer facilitates the employee in this. With a view to the above, social partners are making extra efforts in professionalization and sustainable employability. CAO provisions are being simplified and the administrative burden is being reduced by abolishing the distinction between supplementary professionalization and training commissioned by the employer. The following items are counted towards the professionalization budget: Costs for the basic right in hours; Out-of-pocket costs for all forms of professionalization and training, even if provided internally, provided they can be traced back to development at the individual or team level; Replacement costs – based on average personnel costs – of the person exempted for professionalization or for conducting research; actual replacement must occur. Social partners aim to make the scheme simpler and administratively less burdensome with this expansion of cost items. The intention is not to limit the minimum 6% budget in such a way that professionalization proposals from employees are rejected merely by invoking an exceeding of the professionalization budget. The CAO makes no distinction between women and men. Where 'he', 'his' or 'him' is used, 'she' or 'her' can also be read."], ["Definition of Fulltime employment: An employee whose agreed working hours are 38 hours per week and in Wonen 37 hours. This applies both during and after the employment contract."], ["Transfer: The employee can be transferred in consultation – and in case of temporary transfer after consultation – for business reasons to another location of the employer. Transfer is possible, unless it cannot reasonably be demanded from the employee."], ["Branch closure: In the context of closing a branch, the employer will inform the employees of the branch as early as possible, and at least 1 month before the closure, about the closure of the branch. This contract must at least state: the name of the employer and employee; the date of commencement of employment; the duration of the employment contract; the function; the wage agreed with the employee; the agreed working hours/basic hours; the place where the work is usually performed; that this CAO is applicable."], ["Chain rule for temporary workers: If an employer hires a former temporary worker who immediately prior worked for the employer as a temporary worker, the different temporary agency contracts are considered as one employment contract for the combined duration of the temporary agency contracts. The employee who, after having worked as a temporary worker for the employer, immediately enters into employment with the employer, therefore begins the second chain contract."], ["All-in agreements: It can be agreed with the employee that so-called “all-in” agreements are made for them. In that case, the employment contract states how many hours the employee works on average per week. These are the contract hours or basic hours and serve as the starting point for flexible working."], ["Flexibility - Bandwidth: If flexible working has been agreed, the employer can oblige the employee to work weekly more or fewer hours within a bandwidth of + and - 35 percent of the number of basic hours. This applies in any case a minimum bandwidth of + and - 6 hours of the number of basic hours and a maximum number of scheduled hours of 45 hours per week."], ["Flexibility - Reference period: As the period that serves as the basis for determining to what extent the actual working time deviates from the number of basic hours, the employer sets a period of 12 months. This period is called the reference period. Reference periods may not overlap."], ["Flexibility - Actual working hours: For the determination of whether the employee has been deployed in accordance with their contract hours, various types of hours are counted, the so-called realized hours (see Article 2). Any mandatory presence of the employee required by the employer must be considered as working time, with the exception of an incidental and not pre-planned limited exceeding of the scheduled hours. Such an incidental and/or limited exceeding does not count as realized hours and is therefore not paid."], ["Flexibility - Deviating agreements: Agreements can be made between the employer and the individual employee that deviate from the provisions of Article 4.2 of this CAO."], ["Working and Rest Hours - Five-day work week: The employee cannot be obliged to work on more than five days per week."], ["Working and Rest Hours - Work schedules: When determining the work schedule, the employer will take into account the wishes of the employee as much as possible, unless this cannot reasonably be demanded from the employer. The employee must inform the employer as much as possible in time of special circumstances, for example in the area of childcare, care tasks or care for the sick, so that the employer takes this into account as much as possible in personnel planning."], ["Working and Rest Hours - Non-schedulable days for employees with <= 20 contract hours/week: An employee with a contractual working time per week of 20 hours or less is entitled to a maximum of two non-schedulable days per week. The employee must submit the request in writing two months before the desired start date, or in the case of a new employee before starting employment. The employer can in principle not refuse a request, unless there are compelling business interests, which must be demonstrated by the employer. The employer indicates in writing as soon as possible, but no later than one month before the desired start date, whether the request is honored. If the employer has not responded within this period, the request is deemed to have been honored."], ["Working and Rest Hours - Non-schedulable days for employees with > 20 contract hours/week: For an employee with a contractual working time greater than 20 hours per week, the foregoing also applies, with the understanding that this concerns a maximum of 1 roster-free day per week."], ["Working and Rest Hours - Exclusions for non-schedulable days: Saturday and Sunday are excluded from the non-schedulable days to be agreed upon. The possibility to indicate non-schedulable days does not apply to on-call workers."], ["Working and Rest Hours - Work schedule announcement: The work schedule will be determined and announced at least 2 weeks in advance in consultation with the individual employee, except for an on-call employee. The 2-week period can be deviated from in exceptional cases (e.g., major staff problems, unforeseen circumstances beyond the employer's control)."], ["Working and Rest Hours - Working time and school time: For agreements on working time with an employee who is a student in the context of BBL (Beroepsbegeleidende Leerweg), the hours on the school day are counted as working time. The employer does not have to pay wages for these hours and no holiday rights are accrued. The five-day work week applies to combined working time and school time."], ["Working Hours - Normal working hours: The normal working hours are the times when the enterprise may be open according to the Winkeltijdenwet. For working at certain hours, an allowance may apply (Article 7.1 to Article 7.5 of this CAO)."], ["Working Hours - Normal full-time working hours: The normal working time for the employee with a full-time employment is an average of 38 hours per week, calculated over a period of 12 consecutive months. For employees in Wonen, a full-time employment is an average of 37 hours per week."], ["Job Classification: This CAO has a job evaluation system (FUWAM). There are 9 function levels, A to I, and corresponding wage scales. Each function level contains full functions; each scale can therefore be an end scale. Employees are classified at the correct function level using the Handboek Functie-indeling voor Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle or the online tools made available by the industry organization, such as the website www.functiewijzer.nu. Bijlage 1c contains a summary of the level sheets of that Handbook. The Handbook is available digitally. For employees who disagree with the classification, there is an objection and appeal procedure (see Bijlage 1d). Any disputes about job classification can only be settled on the basis of the (extensive/detailed) Handboek Functie-indeling voor Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle. Employers who wish to apply their own wage structure can submit a request for dispensation to the Sociale Commissie."], ["Working Conditions - RI&E: There is an RI&E for the sector. Employers who use this recognized sector RI&E and are enabled by law to suffice with a lighter, or no assessment, do not have to have the RI&E mandatorily assessed (see also www.rie.nl and www.arboportaal.nl)."], ["Working Conditions - Safety: BBL students, employees younger than 20 years and employees falling into function group A may not work alone in the store premises, with the exception of breaks."], ["Union Facilities - Tax deduction of union fees: Within the applicable laws and regulations, the employer cooperates at the request of the employee with the tax deduction of the union fees paid by the employee."], ["Union Facilities - Union leave: Members of unions that are party to this CAO and board members of Professionals in de Retail are entitled to a maximum of 4 days per year of union leave for attending union meetings, cadre courses and/or training. In addition, leave is granted for participating in CAO negotiations. The employer can apply for wage cost subsidy for both types of leave from the Sociaal Fonds Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle."], ["Life-phase conscious personnel policy - Right to adjust working hours: Employers adjust working hours at the request of employees aged 60 and older. If the employee has a full-time employment and wishes to work 7.6 hours less, the employer compensates the loss of income as follows: The employee works 80% of the initial contract hours; The employer pays 85% of the full-time salary; all other provisions of the CAO apply proportionally, i.e., for the 80% employment. The employee gives up the extra vacation days due to age and length of service to qualify for this scheme."], ["Life-phase conscious personnel policy - Right to preventive consultation with company doctor: Employees aged 46 and older have the right to use the preventive consultation of the company doctor once a year."], ["Life-phase conscious personnel policy - Right to adjust shift work/night shifts: Employees aged 55 and older with demonstrable health complaints, which are established by the company doctor, have the right to request not to work in shift work or drive night shifts. Individually and/or in consultation with the works council, which can be advised by the social partners, the employer makes agreements about the conditions."], ["Life-phase conscious personnel policy - Financial planning: Employees are given the right to have their financial situation made transparent once every 5 years, so that they can better manage the combination of work and private life."], ["Protocolary Provisions - Senior days: If measures are developed in the field of sustainable employability that enable longer working, parties will discuss senior days."], ["Protocolary Provisions - Working hours: CAO parties will conduct research during the term of the CAO into the wishes and needs of employers and employees in the field of working hours, flexibility and employee say over their working hours. An external research agency, jointly selected by CAO parties, will be involved in this research. In addition to this research, a self-scheduling pilot will be set up at at least two large companies (one from Fashion and one from Sport), which will be monitored and guided by CAO parties. The results of this pilot will be evaluated during the negotiations for the next CAO."], ["Protocolary Provisions - CAO modernization: CAO parties will investigate during the term of the CAO how it can be modernized, so that employers and employees feel more connected to it. The other work is then always temporary and for as long as the reason lasts. Temporary lasts a maximum of 3 months, after which extension is possible in consultation between employer and employee. Temporary other work never results in a decrease in wage. If it concerns a higher classified function for longer than one month, the employee receives the scale wage corresponding to that function, insofar as this is higher than their normal wage, for full replacement and responsibilities of that function. For situations where employer and employee disagree on the final function level, an objection and appeal procedure has been developed. Before the employee can submit an appeal to the Sociale Commissie, they must have lodged an objection with their direct supervisor or, in their absence, the employer. The procedure differs for enterprises with or without an employee representation body."], ["Objection procedure (without employee representation body): 1. The employee must submit a written and reasoned objection to their direct supervisor or, in their absence, the employer, within the enterprise where they work, against the procedure followed and/or the final determination of the function level of their function. 2. The employee who disagrees with the function level of their function and/or the procedure followed strives to first find a solution through good consultation with their direct supervisor or, in their absence, the employer. A period of a maximum of 30 days is attached to this good consultation, after the determination of the function level has been made known to the employee by or on behalf of the employer. This period can only be deviated from if both parties agree and a new and/or different period is agreed upon and recorded. 3. After the period of 'good consultation', the employer must revise or maintain their previously taken decision, against which an objection has been made, in writing within a period of 14 days. 4. If the employer has not revised or maintained their original decision within the stipulated period of 14 days, the employee is deemed to have complied with the objection procedure and can appeal to the appeal procedure."], ["Objection procedure (with employee representation body): 1. If the enterprise where the employee works has a Works Council or Personnel Representation, an Internal Joint Disputes Committee will be established. This Internal Joint Disputes Committee will include representatives of the employer and employees. The members of this Internal Joint Disputes Committee may not be directly involved in the determination of the function level of the employee who lodges an objection. 2. The employee must submit a written and reasoned objection to their direct supervisor or, in their absence, the employer, within the enterprise where they work, against the procedure followed and/or the final determination of the function level of their function. 3. The employee who disagrees with their function evaluation and/or the procedure followed strives to first find a solution through good consultation with their direct supervisor or, in their absence, the employer. A period of a maximum of 30 days is attached to this good consultation, after the determination of the function level has been made known to the employee by or on behalf of the employer. This period can only be deviated from if both parties agree and a new and/or different period is agreed upon and recorded. 4. If the good consultation does not lead to a satisfactory solution for the employee, the employee can turn to the Internal Joint Disputes Committee. 5. The Internal Joint Disputes Committee will make a decision within 30 days of receiving the employee's objection, in the form of advice to the employer. This period can only be deviated from if both parties agree and a new and/or different period is agreed upon and recorded. 6. The employer must revise or maintain their previously taken decision, against which an objection has been made, in writing within a period of 14 days after receiving the advice of the Internal Joint Disputes Committee. 7. If the employer has not revised or maintained their original decision within the stipulated period of 14 days, the employee is deemed to have complied with the objection procedure and can appeal to the appeal procedure."], ["Appeal procedure: 1. The employee must appeal within 6 weeks after they could reasonably have become aware of the employer's decision. 2. The appeal is initiated by submitting a written and reasoned, signed and dated appeal to the Sociale Commissie. 3. The appeal can only be lodged against the procedure followed, the final choice for a function family and/or the determination of the corresponding function level. Disputes about the job description must have been settled through 'good' consultation and, if the enterprise where the employee works has an employee representation body, the internal Joint Disputes Committee. 4. The appeal must at least include: a. name, first name(s) (initials) and full address of the employee; b. name and address of the employer; c. if the employee wishes to be assisted: name(s) of the person(s) by whom the employee wishes to be assisted in the appeal procedure; d. the function title, job description and a copy of the employer's letter with the determination of the evaluation of the function, including the function family and the function level of the employee's function; e. a copy of the letter of objection; f. the employer's decision on the 'good consultation' and/or the advice of the internal disputes committee and the related documents; g. as precise a description as possible of the dispute and the claim based thereon."], ["Handling of the appeal: 1. For flexible deployment, this average is the benchmark for determining the bandwidth. See also Article 4.2 of this CAO."], ["Definition of Gerealiseerde uren (Realized hours): Includes: all actually worked hours; paid sick hours; taken and paid holiday hours; taken and paid special leave; compensation of allowances in time; and other existing compensation in hours at individual enterprises."], ["Wage specification: The employer provides a wage specification, possibly digitally, for each wage payment to the employee. In all cases, the employer reimburses any travel and accommodation expenses incurred. This is done retrospectively. If possible, the employee provides proof."], ["Module Wonen - Reimbursements, provisions and anniversary payments - Own transport: If an employee uses their own means of transport at the employer's request, they are reimbursed for mileage costs up to the maximum untaxed mileage allowance permitted by the tax authorities."], ["Module Wonen - Reimbursements, provisions and anniversary payments - Damage: If an employee suffers damage to their glasses, contact lenses, dentures and/or other personal aids during working hours, the employer reimburses the damage. This does not apply if the employee is at fault or if another party covers the costs."], ["Module Wonen - Reimbursements, provisions and anniversary payments - Substitution: If the employer requests the employee to temporarily replace a colleague in a higher function, the employee (the substitute) is entitled to an extra periodic increase within the current salary scale for the duration of the substitution. If the substitute has already reached the maximum of the periodic increases or if their salary is higher than their function classification prescribes, they receive an amount equal to twice the difference between function year 23/0 and 23/1 within their current salary scale for the duration of the substitution."], ["Module Wonen - Reimbursements, provisions and anniversary payments - Service anniversary: The employee receives one gross monthly salary for 25 and 40 years of service, paid in accordance with fiscal regulations."], ["Module Juweliers en Parfumerieën - Job classification and wage structure: Before 1 August 2017, employers classified their employees' functions in FUWAM. From this date, the loongebouw Mode of CAO Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle applies to new employees. Current employees will be classified into the new wage structure with their increased wage (as of 1 July or 1 August 2017), including any increase to compensate for the abolition of the late-night shopping allowance, and if the compensation for the reduction of the Sunday allowance has been paid in money, also with this increased wage. This means that the wage will almost always fall between two steps. When reaching an experience year, the step will be made to the next higher step, if the employee is entitled to a step increase."], ["Module Juweliers en Parfumerieën - Wage: As of 1 August 2017, the wages of employees who have not yet reached the new maximum of their scale will be increased by 0.89%. Employees who receive the Wettelijk Minimumloon will not receive this increase on 1 August, because their wage will already be increased by 0.89% on 1 July 2017, the percentage by which the statutory minimum wages are increased as of 1 July 2017. Collectively granted wage increases from 1 January 2016 can be deducted from the wage increase as of 1 August 2017. As of 1 January 2018, employees will receive a wage increase equal to the percentage by which the statutory minimum wages increase, with a minimum of 0.4%. Employees who earn more than the maximum of their scale will only receive an increase on the part of their salary up to this maximum."], ["Module Juweliers en Parfumerieën - Substitution allowance: The employee who substitutes in a higher function is entitled to an allowance of 15% of the amount at zero function years in the scale in which the function to be replaced is classified, for the duration of the substitution. The temporary new salary (salary plus 15%) cannot, however, exceed the salary of the functionary being replaced. There is no entitlement to this allowance for replacement due to vacation and when the replacement lasts less than one calendar month."], ["Module Juweliers en Parfumerieën - Late-night shopping allowance: As of 1 July 2017, the allowance for working on late-night shopping evenings is abolished. The employee who works on late-night shopping evenings, remains willing to do so and receives an allowance for this, will receive compensation for the abolition of the late-night shopping allowance in the form of a wage increase equal to the percentage they earned in late-night shopping allowances from 1 July 2016 to 1 July 2017 (this is the reference period). Example calculation: 3 hours of work on a late-night shopping evening at 33.33% allowance equals 1 hourly wage. The CAO has a 38-hour work week, so an hourly wage for a full-time employee is 1/38 = 2.63%. For a part-time employee, it should not be divided by 38, but by the number of hours per week. The reference period is one year, which is 52 weeks. The calculation for a full-time employee is then x/52 * 2.63%, where x = the number of late-night shopping evenings worked in the reference period. Example: a full-time employee who worked 26 late-night shopping evenings receives a wage increase of 26/52 * 2.63% = 1.32%."], ["Table: Loontabel Mode, Verf en Woninginrichting per 1 juli 2016 ["Criteria for performance-based pay (Bijlage 1d): 1. Function content must be fixed: There must be clarity and agreement between the supervisor and employee about the employee's function (tasks and responsibilities), recorded in a written job description. 2. At least 1 (preferably 2) appraisal conversation(s) per year: The direct supervisor conducts at least 1 conversation per year with the employee about their functioning. This conversation covers expectations for the coming year, current functioning in relation to agreements, and a judgment on functioning with consequences for remuneration. If 2 conversations per year, one is a performance review (equal partners, focus on expectations and current functioning) and one is an appraisal (supervisor gives judgment, limited employee input). 3. The appraisal procedure must be written and known to employees: The procedure, purpose, meaning, and consequences of the conversation must be in writing and known to the employee, and agreed upon by the works council if present. Criteria for appraisal must be clear beforehand. Possible appraisal outcomes (e.g., insufficient, moderate, normal/good, very good, excellent) must be clearly described with clear salary consequences. Specifically, clear definitions for 'sufficient' and 'insufficient' are required for salary consequences. The judgment and other agreements made during appraisal conversations must be recorded in writing. The appraisal system is regularly evaluated by management (and representative consultation)."]], "pension_information": [["Collective Industry Regulations - Pension: The employer is obliged to be affiliated with the Bedrijfstakpensioenfonds Detailhandel (Industry Pension Fund Retail) on the basis of a mandatory declaration from the Minister of Social Affairs and Employment, unless they have been dispensed or exempted. Conditions may be attached to the exemption. The Stichting Bedrijfstakpensioenfonds Detailhandel is responsible for the execution of the pension scheme. The board of the foundation has entrusted the management and administration of the pension scheme to TKP in Groningen. This body also handles the collection of premiums for the pension fund. Further information about the pension scheme can be obtained from TKP, Postbus 1233, 9701 BE Groningen, telephone 050 - 5225098. The Bedrijfstakpensioenfonds Detailhandel also has a website: www.pensioendetailhandel.nl. The above article on pension does not apply to the woondetailhandel."], ["Life-phase conscious personnel policy - Right to adjust working hours: The employer deducts 100% employee premium and pays 100% employee and employer premium to the Bedrijfspensioenfonds."], ["Protocolary Provisions - Pension: CAO parties will conduct research during the term of the CAO into a sustainable and future-proof pension system, explicitly including individual pension. Developments and research, both central and sectoral, will be taken into account in the research."], ["Module Wonen - Pension: The employer is obliged to be affiliated with Bedrijfstakpensioenfonds Wonen (Industry Pension Fund Wonen) on the basis of a mandatory declaration from the Minister of Social Affairs and Employment, unless they have been dispensed or exempted. Conditions may be attached to the exemption. Addresses: Pensioenfonds Detailhandel (Postbus 1233, 9701 BE Groningen, 050 - 5225098), Pensioenfonds Wonen (Postbus 3183, 3502 GD Utrecht, 088 - 0084002)."]], "leave_information": [["Definition of Feestdagen (Public holidays): Considered as public holidays are: Nieuwjaarsdag; both Easter days; Ascension Day; both Pentecost days; both Christmas days; the day designated by the government on which Koningsdag is celebrated; lustrum celebration of Bevrijdingsdag (once every 5 years in years that are a multiple of 5)."], ["Vacation year: The vacation year coincides with the calendar year, unless other agreements have been made with the works council. Over this period, the employee accrues vacation rights."], ["Vacation hours with retention of wage: The employee is entitled to vacation with retention of wage for 182.4 hours per year (based on an average 38-hour work week or an average 7.6-hour workday). For Wonen, this is 185 hours (based on an average 37-hour work week or an average 7.4-hour workday). Vacation rights are deemed to accrue proportionally over the year. An employee who is employed for only part of the vacation year is entitled to a proportional part of the vacation mentioned in this article."], ["Accrual of vacation hours: For the accrual of vacation hours, the number of agreed contract hours (or basic hours) is generally used as a basis. If the employee works more than their basic hours during a vacation year, vacation hours are also accrued over the excess, unless the extra hours are taken as time off in accordance with Article 4."], ["Determination of vacation hours to be taken: When determining the number of hours to be taken for a holiday (day), the number of hours the employee would work according to their schedule is generally used as a basis. If no schedule has been determined, the number of vacation hours to be taken in the relevant week is equal to the number of hours per week that the employee has worked on average in the last 13 weeks preceding the holiday. If that reference period is not representative, the last 52 weeks preceding the day of the holiday are used."], ["Consecutive vacation: The employee is entitled to two consecutive weeks of vacation per calendar year."], ["Vacation during sickness: If the employee has received permission to go on vacation during the illness period (see also the absence regulations in Bijlage 2 of this CAO), these days will be deducted from the vacation balance."], ["Expiration of statutory vacation days: Statutory vacation days expire 6 months after the calendar year in which they were acquired, but the employer may, in consultation with the works council, deviate from this expiration period and then apply the old expiration period of 5 years. The agreed regulation then applies to all employees."], ["Collective vacation days: The employer may, in consultation with the works council, designate a maximum of 2 days per year as mandatory collective vacation days for the enterprise. Deviations per location and site are possible."], ["Deviation in consultation with the works council: Further implementation rules can be established by the employer in consultation with the works council."], ["Extra vacation hours due to age: An employee aged: 50 to 55 years is entitled to extra vacation hours equal to 1/5 of the number of basic hours per week; 55 to 60 years is entitled to extra vacation hours equal to 2/5 of the number of basic hours per week; 60 years or older is entitled to extra vacation hours equal to 3/5 of the number of basic hours per week, unless there is an adjustment of working hours in accordance with Article 10. In that case, these extra vacation hours lapse. The employee accrues the extra vacation hours per vacation year."], ["Extra vacation hours due to length of service: An employee with a service period of: 25 to 40 years is entitled to extra vacation hours equal to 2/5 of the number of basic hours per week; 40 years or more is entitled to extra vacation hours equal to 4/5 of the number of basic hours per week. The employee accrues the extra vacation hours per vacation year."], ["Overlap of extra vacation hours: If the extra vacation hours scheme due to age coincides with the extra vacation hours scheme due to the length of service, only the scheme that is most favorable to the employee applies."], ["Retention of wage for extra vacation hours: The employee retains the right to their normal wage for the extra vacation hours."], ["Holiday allowance: The holiday allowance year runs from 1 June to 31 May. The employer may, in consultation with the works council, agree on a different holiday allowance year. The employee who has been employed for the entire holiday allowance year receives an amount of holiday allowance of 8% of the actually earned wage in the holiday allowance year, paid no later than June."], ["Special Leave: Special leave enables an employee to attend special events, particularly in their private sphere. For a part-timer, the right to leave applies to the hours they would normally work on the day(s) concerned. In addition to the statutory leave regulations from the Wet Arbeid & Zorg, the employee is entitled to special leave with retention of wage in the following cases:"], ["Special Leave - Marriage: for their marriage: on the day of the marriage and the day before or after; for attending the marriage of one of their children (including step- and foster children), brothers and sisters as well as one of the parents (in-law): the day of the marriage; for their 25th, 40th, 50th and 60th wedding anniversary and that of their parents and parents-in-law: the day of the anniversary or the day on which the celebration takes place."], ["Special Leave - Death: for the death of the spouse/partner or of their own cohabiting children: from the day of death up to and including the day of the funeral; for the death of one of the parents, parents-in-law, own non-cohabiting children, foster or step-children: the day on which the funeral takes place and the day of death or a day between the death and the funeral; for attending the funeral of grandparents, partner's grandparents, grandchildren, brothers, sisters, brothers-in-law and sisters-in-law: the day of the funeral."], ["Special Leave - Service anniversary: for the employee's 25th, 40th and 50th service anniversary: 1 day."], ["Special Leave - Relocation: for relocation: the day of relocation."], ["Special Leave - Job application: if the employment is terminated by the employer: for a reasonable period for applying for a new job and verbally explaining the application."], ["Special Leave - Medical reason: The starting point is that the employee makes appointments in their own time. If that is not possible, there is a right to special leave for a mutually agreed reasonable period: for undergoing a mandatory medical examination; and for necessary visits by the employee to the doctor, dentist or specialist."], ["Special Leave - Sustainable cohabitation: An employee who has already entered into a legally registered partnership or a sustainable cohabitation is treated equally to a married employee."], ["Incapacity for Work - Absence regulations: In case of illness or accident preventing the employee from working, the absence regulations established in the enterprise must be followed. If such regulations are absent, the standard absence regulations Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle (Bijlage 2 of this CAO) apply. Changes to these regulations can be agreed upon with the works council."], ["Incapacity for Work - Continued wage payment: In case of incapacity for work, the employee is entitled to continued wage payment of 100% of the gross wage for the first 26 weeks. In the subsequent period of 26 weeks (week 27 to 52), 90% of the gross wage, but at least the statutory minimum wage, is paid. In the period of 26 weeks thereafter (week 53 to 78), 80% of the gross wage is paid. In the period of 26 weeks thereafter (week 79 to 104), the statutory wage payment obligation of 70% of the gross wage applies."], ["Incapacity for Work - Waiting days: Per illness case, 1 waiting day can be deducted. Periods of illness are aggregated if they follow each other with an interruption of less than four weeks. The employer does not have to pay wage for a waiting day. The employer is allowed to offset the waiting day with above-statutory vacation hours granted to the employee, with the wage or with compensation days/hours, unless otherwise agreed with the employee representation body. A maximum of four waiting days per year can be offset with vacation hours or deducted from the wage. If the occupational health physician/company doctor (an occupational health service physician) has diagnosed a chronic illness in the employee, the employer will be extremely reluctant to deduct waiting days."], ["Incapacity for Work - Determination of sick pay and waiting day: When determining the number of paid sick hours per day and the number of hours for the waiting day, the number of hours the employee would have worked according to their schedule is used as a basis. If the employee does not work in a store and the working hours cannot be determined in advance due to external circumstances (e.g., for drivers), the number of contract hours per week can be used instead of the scheduled hours. If no schedule has been determined, the number of hours the employee has worked on average per day in the last 13 weeks preceding the incapacity for work is used as a basis. If that reference period is not representative in the employer's opinion, the last 52 weeks preceding the day of illness are used."], ["Incapacity for Work - Consecutive illness cases: Illness cases that follow each other within a period of 4 weeks are counted as one illness case, regardless of the cause of the illness."], ["Incapacity for Work - Offsetting: Any benefits under social insurance laws that will be paid through the employer will be deducted from the wage payments."], ["Incapacity for Work - End of wage payment: The wage payments mentioned in this article end at the end of the employment contract of the employee concerned."], ["Incapacity for Work - Transfer of rights: If the employer is obliged to continue paying wages during incapacity for work, the employee must transfer their rights against third parties (including those of the UWV) due to wage loss due to incapacity for work to the employer."], ["Incapacity for Work - Expense reimbursement and incapacity: If the incapacity for work lasts longer than one month consecutively, the employer is not obliged to pay expense reimbursements directly related to the work after that month."], ["Standard sick leave regulations (Bijlage 2) - Definition of sick leave: Sickness absence refers to the absence of employees from the workplace due to unfitness for performing (adapted) work due to illness or accident. This means that sickness absence is only accepted for a medical reason."], ["Standard sick leave regulations (Bijlage 2) - Reporting sick on time: If you cannot work due to illness, adhere to the following rules: Report sick by phone before the start of working hours, but no later than 9:00 AM, to your employer (Mr./Ms. [name], phone [number]). In case of their absence, to [name], phone [number]. If you are unable to do so yourself, have someone else call. If you become ill during the day and only have to start later, for example for an afternoon or evening shift, report sick as soon as possible. If you become ill during working hours, report this to the aforementioned persons before going home. Note: In connection with the right to continued wage payment or a sickness benefit from the Sickness Benefits Act, it is important that you report sick on the first day of illness. Example: you are scheduled to work on Thursday, Friday and Saturday. On Monday you become ill. When reporting sick, agreements can be made about control and about the day and time when we will have weekly contact with you. (*04 = Privacy law applies to these questions. You do not have to provide this information to us (employer or colleagues). You must provide this information to the ARBO-dienst/company doctor. Without your permission, the ARBO-dienst/company doctor will not pass on confidential information to us. Personal and confidential information you provide to us will, of course, be treated with appropriate confidentiality and discretion.) We will inform the ARBO-dienst/company doctor about this; We also ask you to report by phone when you go for a check-up to the ARBO-dienst/company doctor. We also want you to let us know what advice you have received regarding a possible return to work."], ["Standard sick leave regulations (Bijlage 2) - Control: During illness, you must be reachable during working hours for a control visit by the employer and the ARBO-dienst or the company doctor ([name of ARBO-dienst/company doctor]). Therefore, it is necessary that you enable them to visit you at home or at the nursing address. If – while you are at home – something unusual is happening (for example, the doorbell is defective or no one is home to open the door), take measures to ensure that the employer or ARBO-dienst/company doctor can still access the home; The control can also be carried out by phone. Therefore, you must be reachable by phone at home or at the nursing address on the number you provided when reporting sick; You must stay at home (or at the nursing address) until the first contact with the ARBO-dienst/company doctor has taken place. However, you must cancel the appointment by phone with the ARBO-dienst (at least 24 hours in advance); If you are prevented (for example, because you are bedridden), you must report this immediately yourself and make a new appointment; Your travel expenses will be reimbursed based on public transport second class, unless these costs are normally incurred for commuting; You can also make an appointment with the ARBO-dienst/company doctor yourself. This is advisable if you foresee absence or if you want to discuss your working conditions with an ARBO expert."], ["Standard sick leave regulations (Bijlage 2) - Vacation or stay abroad during illness: If you become ill during your vacation, you must report this as soon as possible (by phone, telegram, fax or e-mail) to the employer, stating your vacation address. This medical certificate must state the duration, nature and treatment of the incapacity for work. Based on this certificate, the ARBO-dienst/company doctor advises the employer on the return of vacation days. If you wish to go on vacation during the illness period, you need a 'declaration of no objection' from the ARBO-dienst/company doctor. Together with that declaration, you then submit a request to go on vacation. We will then decide whether to grant the vacation. Permission is given if the ARBO-dienst/company doctor believes that the vacation does not hinder recovery.
